### canonical-a-3e8a992b0ac317fc

DCM severity and rate of change determine management. Mild DCM is not automatically an emergency and may, following specialist assessment and shared decision-making, be managed with a supervised structured rehabilitation trial. Moderate/severe DCM and deterioration during non-operative care require surgical assessment under the AO Spine guideline.

- Primary class: **A. Emergency / red flag**
- Severity: **CRITICAL**
- Verification: **VERIFIED**
- Local service status: **NOT A LOCAL SERVICE CLAIM**

- Exact supporting section: Summary of recommendations
- Mapping rationale: The claim directly concerns the guideline recommendation for surgical intervention in moderate or severe DCM.

### canonical-d-eaef70a3464834ad

Clinical reasoning should use the ICHD-3 criteria and a full headache/neck assessment. CFRT and upper-cervical examination may contribute but are not definitive. Diagnostic blockade is one possible ICHD-3 item demonstrating causation and is a specialist procedure, not a routine physiotherapy confirmation test.

### canonical-e-793c603c45a57890

No NICE guideline cited here supplies a mechanical-neck-pain dosing pathway. Medication selection, dose, duration and gastroprotection require an individual prescriber assessment and current prescribing guidance. NG193 recommendations about antidepressants, opioids, gabapentinoids, benzodiazepines, NSAIDs and paracetamol apply specifically to management of **chronic primary pain** and must not be extrapolated automatically to all mechanical neck pain.
